\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}\cdot\beta+(3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8)+4
. As
\alpha+\beta
is required to be even in this case, all the summands are integers. Furthermore, as
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required in this case, all the summands are non-negative, and the summand
4
is positive. Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}=2(\beta-1)\gamma+\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}\cdot(\beta-1)+2
. As
\alpha+\beta
is required to be odd in this case, all the summands are integers. Furthermore, all of them are non-negative, and the summand
2
is positive. Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is larger by
\beta-2\gamma-3
than the one in the fourth case. As
\alpha+\beta
is odd in both cases,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is also an integer in this case. Furthermore, as
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
is required in this case,
\beta-2\gamma-3\geq 0
, and the value of
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
in this case is also positive.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
All the summands are non-negative and
\frac{\alpha}{2}
is positive. Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)>0
. Furthermore, as
\alpha
is divisible by four, all the summands are integers, and thus
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
All the summands are non-negative and
\frac{\alpha}{2}
is positive. Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)>0
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{(\alpha+1)\cdot(\alpha+3)}{8}
.
The first two summands are clearly integers. As
\alpha
is odd in this case,
\alpha+1
and
\alpha+3
are both divisible by two. Furthermore, as
\alpha+1
and
\alpha+3
differ by exactly two, one of them is also divisible by four. Therefore,
(\alpha+3)\cdot(\alpha+1)
is divisible by eight, and the last summand is also an integer.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
All the summands are non-negative and
\frac{\alpha}{2}
is positive. Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)>0
.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{(\alpha+2)^{2}}{8}
. As
\alpha+2
is divisible by four in this case,
(\alpha+2)^{2}
is divisible by eight, and thus all the summands are integers.

Lemma 8.9
.

For
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, such that
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\left\lceil 2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\right\rceil
.

Proof 8.10
.

If
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
, one of the last three cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
Note that in these cases
0\leq\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2})<1
.
As
Lemma

8.7
implies that
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is an integer, we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\left\lceil 2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\right\rceil
.

Lemma 8.11
.

For any
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+\gamma
.

Proof 8.12
.

Since
\mathcal{F}(1,0,0)=1
due to
Lemma

8.3
and
\mathcal{F}(2,1,0)=2
due to
Lemma

8.5
, the claim is obvious for
(1,0,0),(2,1,0)\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. All remaining
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
have
\beta\geq 2
. We use this inequality to simplify
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
:

Case 1:
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq 4\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2\geq\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+2
. By computing the derivative, we see that this is minimized for
\beta=(\alpha+1)/2
. Plugging this in, we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq 2\gamma+(\alpha-1)^{2}/8+2
. Finally,
(\alpha-1)^{2}/8+2\geq\alpha/2
yields the claimed bound.

Case 2:
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq 4\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(\tfrac{\beta}{2}+2)-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma=\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+2
. From here we can argue as in the previous case.

Case 3: Since in this case
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8\geq\beta+8
, we must have
\beta\geq 4
. We use this to bound
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq 8\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(\beta+8)-\alpha-6\gamma-4\geq\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\alpha+4
. By computing the derivative, we see that this is minimized for
\beta=\alpha/2+1
. Plugging this in, we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\gamma+(\alpha-2)^{2}/8+4
. Finally,
(\alpha-2)^{2}/8+4\geq\alpha/2
yields the claimed bound.

Case 4: Using
\tfrac{\beta}{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}
we see that the function value in this case it at least the function value in case 1, so we can argue as in case 1.

Case 5: Since
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
we have
\beta\geq 3
. Using this, we get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq 6\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\tfrac{3}{2}
. Using
\tfrac{3}{2}\beta\geq\tfrac{9}{2}\geq\tfrac{3}{2}+2
we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\gamma+\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+2
. From here we can argue as in case 1.

Cases 6-8: Clear since
2\gamma^{2}\geq\gamma
.

Lemma 8.13
.

Let

f_{2}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\coloneqq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma,

f_{4}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\coloneqq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2},

and

f_{5}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\coloneqq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}.

For every
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=

\begin{cases}f_{4}(\alpha-1,\beta,\gamma),&\text{if $\alpha+\beta$ is even, $\alpha>\beta$, and $\beta<\gamma+2$;}\\
f_{2}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma),&\text{if $\alpha+\beta$ is even, $3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8$, and ($\alpha=\beta$ or $\beta\geq\gamma+2$);}\\
f_{5}(\alpha,\beta-1,\gamma),&\text{if $\alpha+\beta$ is even, $2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6$, and $3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8$;}\\
f_{4}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma),&\text{if $\alpha+\beta$ is odd and $\beta<2\gamma+3$;}\\
f_{5}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma),&\text{if $\alpha+\beta$ is odd, $2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6$, and $\beta\geq 2\gamma+3$;}\\
f_{5}(\alpha,2\gamma+\frac{\alpha+((\alpha-1)\bmod 4)+3}{2},\gamma),&\text{if $2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6$.}\\
\end{cases}

Furthermore, if
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
, then
2\gamma+\frac{\alpha+((\alpha-1)\bmod 4)+3}{2}
is a positive integer and
2\gamma+\frac{\alpha+((\alpha-1)\bmod 4)+3}{2}\leq\beta-1
.

In other words, Case 1 from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
can be defined via Case 4 for a smaller triple
(\alpha-1,\beta,\gamma)
,
Case 3 from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
can be defined via Case 5 for a smaller triple
(\alpha,\beta-1,\gamma)
,
and Cases 6, 7, and 8 can be defined via Case 5 for a smaller triple
(\alpha,2\gamma+\frac{\alpha+((\alpha-1)\bmod 4)+3}{2},\gamma)
.

Proof 8.14
.

We consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
As
\alpha>\beta
in this case, we have
\alpha-1\geq\beta
.
Furthermore, as
\alpha+\beta
is even,
(\alpha-1)+\beta
is odd.
As
\beta<\gamma+2
in this case, we have
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
Therefore, triple
(\alpha-1,\beta,\gamma)
satisfies the conditions of Case 4 from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.
Finally, we have

\displaystyle f_{4}(\alpha-1,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{(\alpha-1)\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma).

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
This case is trivial.

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
In this case
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8\geq\beta+6\gamma+8
, so
\beta-1\geq(3\gamma+4)-1\geq 2\gamma+3
.
Furthermore, as
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha+(\beta-1)
is odd.
Moreover,
2(\beta-1)<2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
is required in this case.
Therefore, triple
(\alpha,\beta-1,\gamma)
satisfies the conditions of Case 5 from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.
Finally, we have

\displaystyle f_{5}(\alpha,\beta-1,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2(\beta-1)\gamma+\frac{\alpha(\beta-1)}{2}-\frac{(\beta-1)^{2}}{2}+2(\beta-1)-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma).

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
This case is trivial.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
This case is trivial.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
Here we consider all cases
6
through
8
together.
Define
x\coloneqq(((\alpha-1)\bmod 4)+3)/2
.
Denote
\beta^{\prime}\coloneqq\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+x
.
It is easy to see that
\beta^{\prime}
is a positive integer.
Furthermore,
2\alpha+2\beta^{\prime}=2\alpha+\alpha+4\gamma+(\alpha-1)+3=2\bmod 4
, hence
\alpha+\beta^{\prime}
is odd.
By the case condition we have
\alpha+\beta\geq 2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
, therefore
\alpha\geq\beta\geq 4\gamma+6\geq 6
.
Thus, we have
\beta^{\prime}=\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+x\geq 2\gamma+3
.
Furthermore,
\beta^{\prime}<\beta\leq\alpha
because
x\leq 3
and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
by the case assumption.
Finally,
2\beta^{\prime}\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
as
x\leq 3
.
Therefore, triple
(\alpha,\beta^{\prime},\gamma)
satisfies the conditions of Case 5 from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.
We obtain

\displaystyle f_{5}(\alpha,\beta^{\prime},\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta^{\prime}\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta^{\prime}}{2}-\frac{\beta^{\prime 2}}{2}+2\beta^{\prime}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2(\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+x)\gamma+\frac{\alpha(\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+x)}{2}-\frac{(\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+x)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle+2(\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+x)-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+(2x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma),

where the last inequality can be checked for each value of
\alpha\bmod 4
.

(a)

\alpha=0\bmod 4
. In this case
x=(3+3)/2=3
. Hence,
2x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2}=0
.

(b)

\alpha=1\bmod 4
. In this case
x=(0+3)/2=3/2
. Hence,
2x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2}=3/8
.

(c)

\alpha=2\bmod 4
. In this case
x=(1+3)/2=2
. Hence,
2x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2}=1/2
.

(d)

\alpha=3\bmod 4
. In this case
x=(2+3)/2=5/2
. Hence,
2x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2}=3/8
.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

Lemma 8.15
.

For
\alpha\geq 2,\beta=2
, and
\gamma=0
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\left\lceil(\alpha+\beta)/2\right\rceil
.

Proof 8.16
.

If
\alpha
is even, we have that
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and
\beta\geq\gamma+2
.
Hence, the second case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We substitute and get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\alpha/2+1=\left\lceil(\alpha+\beta)/2\right\rceil
.

If, on the other hand,
\alpha
is odd, we have that
\alpha+\beta
is odd and
\beta<\gamma+3
.
Hence, the fourth case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We substitute and get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\alpha/2+3/2=\left\lceil(\alpha+\beta)/2\right\rceil
.

Lemma 8.17
.

For
\alpha=2,\beta=2
, and
\gamma\geq 0
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\gamma+2
.

Proof 8.18
.

In this case
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and
\alpha=\beta
.
Hence, the second case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We substitute and get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\gamma+2
.

Lemma 8.19
.

For
\alpha\geq 3
,
\beta=2
, and
\gamma\geq 1
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma+2+\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-1}{2}\right\rfloor
.

Proof 8.20
.

Consider two cases.

1.

\alpha
is even.
Then
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
. So the first case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
applies.
We substitute and get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=4\gamma+\alpha-2+1-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2=2\gamma+\frac{\alpha}{2}+1=2\gamma+2+\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-1}{2}\right\rfloor
.

2.

\alpha
is odd.
Then
\alpha+\beta
is odd and
\beta<2\gamma+3
. So the fourth case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
applies.
We substitute and get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=4\gamma+\alpha-2+2-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}=2\gamma+2+\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-1}{2}\right\rfloor
.

Lemma 8.21
.

For
\alpha=\beta\geq 3
and
\gamma\geq 1
we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq(2\alpha-3)\cdot\gamma+\alpha
.

Proof 8.22
.

We need to prove
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\alpha,\gamma)\geq 2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma
. We consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
This case requires
\alpha>\beta
which contradicts the fact that
\alpha=\beta
.

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma=2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma
.

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
In this case
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required which implies
\alpha\geq 3\gamma+4
because
\alpha=\beta
. Thus,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4=2\alpha\gamma+2\alpha-6\gamma-4\geq 2\alpha\gamma+\alpha+(3\gamma+4)-6\gamma-4=2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma
.

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
This case requires
\alpha+\beta
being odd which contradicts the fact that
\alpha=\beta
.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
This case requires
\alpha+\beta
being odd which contradicts the fact that
\alpha=\beta
.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
is required which implies
\alpha>4\gamma+6
because
\alpha=\beta
.
Thus,
\frac{\alpha}{2}\geq 2\gamma+2
.
Therefore, we get
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma)\cdot(\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma-2)+\gamma+2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma\geq 2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma
.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}
is larger by
\frac{3}{8}
than the one in the sixth case while
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
still holds.
Thus, the inequality follows from the sixth case.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}
is larger by
\frac{1}{2}
than the one in the sixth case while
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
still holds.
Thus, the inequality follows from the sixth case.

Lemma 8.23
.

For
\alpha>\beta\geq 3
and
\gamma\geq 1
we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+2
.

Proof 8.24
.

We want to prove

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+2=2\beta\gamma+\beta-2\gamma+1.

(*)

Consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
.
In terms of
\alpha
this is a linear function with slope
\frac{\beta-1}{2}>0
.
As
\alpha>\beta
and
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case, we obtain
\alpha\geq\beta+2
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\mathcal{F}(\beta+2,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{(\beta+2)\cdot\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta+2}{2}-2\gamma+2=2\beta\gamma+\beta-2\gamma+1
.

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
This case requires
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
. Since
\alpha>\beta
, we have
\beta\geq\gamma+2
. The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the previous case by
\beta-\gamma-2\geq 0
. As the only condition that we used in the previous case to prove (*) was the fact that
\alpha+\beta
is even, the same arguments apply here.

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the first case by
\frac{5}{2}\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-6=(3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8)+\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\alpha-\beta)+(2\gamma+2)
, which is nonnegative due to the fact that
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
in this case.
As the only condition that we used in the first case to prove (*) was the fact that
\alpha+\beta
is even, the same arguments apply here.

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
In terms of
\alpha
, the value of
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
in this case is a linear function with slope
\frac{\beta-1}{2}>0
.
Since
\alpha\geq\beta+1
, we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\mathcal{F}(\beta+1,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{(\beta+1)\cdot\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\beta+1}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}=2\beta\gamma+\beta-2\gamma+1
.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the previous case by
\beta-2\gamma-3
, which is nonnegative due to the fact that
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
in this case. Therefore, the same arguments as in the previous case apply.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
. This is monotonically increasing in
\alpha
(for positive
\alpha
).
Furthermore, in this case
\alpha+4\gamma+6<2\beta<\alpha+\beta
is required, which yields
\beta-4\gamma>6
.
Since
\alpha\geq\beta+1
, we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\mathcal{F}(\beta+1,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+(\beta+1)\gamma+\frac{(\beta+1)^{2}}{8}+\frac{\beta+1}{2}=\frac{(\beta-4\gamma)(\beta-4\gamma-2)}{8}+(2\gamma-\frac{3}{8})+2\beta\gamma+\beta-2\gamma+1\geq 2\beta\gamma+\beta-2\gamma+1
.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}
is larger by
\frac{3}{8}
than the one in the sixth case while
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
still holds.
Thus, the inequality follows from the sixth case.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}
is larger by
\frac{1}{2}
than the one in the sixth case while
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
still holds.
Thus, the inequality follows from the sixth case.

Lemma 8.25
.

For
\alpha>\beta\geq 3
and
\gamma\geq 1
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq(\alpha-2\ell+1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+\ell
where
\ell\coloneqq\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)
.

Proof 8.26
.

We need to prove

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}(\alpha-2\ell+1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+\ell

\displaystyle=(\alpha+1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)-(4\gamma+1)\ell

\displaystyle=(\alpha+1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)-(4\gamma+1)\cdot(\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1))

\displaystyle=(\alpha+1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+(4\gamma+1)\cdot(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)-(4\gamma+1)\cdot\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma).

By rearranging, we simplify our proof goal to

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{4\gamma+2}\cdot((\alpha+1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+(4\gamma+1)\cdot(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1+(4\gamma+1)\cdot(\beta-1))

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma.

(*)

We consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
As
\alpha>\beta
by the lemma statement, and
\alpha+\beta
is required to be even in this case, we get
\alpha\geq\beta+2
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\alpha(\beta-2)}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{(\beta+2)\cdot(\beta-2)}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma,

thus proving (*).

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the previous case by
\beta-\gamma-2
, which is nonnegative as in this case either
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
is required, and by lemma statement
\alpha>\beta
.
As the only condition that we used in the previous case to prove (*) was the fact that
\alpha+\beta
is even, the same arguments apply here.

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the first case by
\frac{5}{2}\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-6=(3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8)+\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+2\gamma+2
, which is nonnegative as
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required in this case.
As the only condition that we used in the first case to prove (*) was the fact that
\alpha+\beta
is even, the same arguments apply here.

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
As
\alpha>\beta
by the lemma statement, we get
\alpha\geq\beta+1
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\alpha(\beta-2)}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{(\beta+1)\cdot(\beta-2)}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma+\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma,

thus proving (*).

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the previous case by
\beta-2\gamma-3
, which is nonnegative due to the fact that
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
is required in this case.
Therefore, the same arguments as in the previous case apply.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
As
\alpha+4\gamma+6<2\beta\leq\alpha+\beta
is required in this case, we get
\beta-4\gamma-2\geq 0
.
Furthermore, as
\alpha>\beta
by the lemma statement, we get
\alpha\geq\beta+1
.
Combining these facts together, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\gamma^{2}+(\beta+1)\cdot\gamma+\frac{(\beta+1)^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma+(2\gamma^{2}-\beta\gamma+3\gamma-\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{(\beta+1)^{2}}{8})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma+(\frac{(\beta-4\gamma)\cdot(\beta-4\gamma-2)}{8}+2\gamma+\frac{1}{8})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}+2\beta\gamma-2\gamma,

thus proving (*).

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}
is different by
\frac{3}{8}
than the one in the sixth case, and the same arguments apply.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
In this case the value of
\mathcal{F}
is different by
\frac{1}{2}
than the one in the sixth case, and the same arguments apply.

Lemma 8.27
.

For
\alpha\geq\beta=3
and
\gamma\geq 1
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq 2\gamma+\alpha+1
.

Proof 8.28
.

We consider four cases.

1.

\alpha=\beta
.

In this case
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and
\alpha=\beta
, thus the second case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma=3\gamma+3\geq 2\gamma+4=2\gamma+\alpha+1
.

2.

\alpha\neq\beta
and
\alpha
is even.

In this case
\alpha+\beta
is odd and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
Thus, the fourth case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}=4\gamma+\alpha\geq 2\gamma+\alpha+1
.

3.

\alpha\neq\beta
,
\alpha
is odd, and
\gamma=1
.

In this case
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and
\beta\geq\gamma+2
.
Thus, the second case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma=\alpha+3=2\gamma+\alpha+1
.

4.

\alpha\neq\beta
,
\alpha
is odd, and
\gamma\geq 2
.

In this case
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
Thus, the first case from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
is applicable.
We obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2=4\gamma+\alpha-1\geq 2\gamma+\alpha+1
.

Lemma 8.29
.

For
\alpha\geq\beta\geq 3
,
\gamma\geq 1
, and
k\geq 2
we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq(\beta-2k+1)\cdot(k+2\gamma)+k
.

Proof 8.30
.

We want to prove

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}(\beta-2k+1)\cdot(k+2\gamma)+k=-2k^{2}+(\beta-4\gamma+2)k+(2\beta\gamma+2\gamma).

(*)

The right-hand side of (*) is a quadratic function
k
with a negative coefficient for
k^{2}
.
It achieves the maximum value for
k=k_{\max}\coloneqq\frac{\beta-4\gamma+2}{4}
.
As
k_{\max}
may be fractional, the maximum value over integer values of
k
is achieved for the closest integer to
k_{\max}
.
That is,
k^{\prime}_{\max}\coloneqq\left\lfloor\frac{\beta-4\gamma+3}{4}\right\rfloor=\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil-\gamma
.
Furthermore, as we have
k\geq 2
, the maximum over possible values of
k
is achieved for
k=k_{0}\coloneqq\max\{k^{\prime}_{\max},2\}
.
Hence,
-2k_{0}^{2}+(\beta-4\gamma+2)k_{0}+(2\beta\gamma+2\gamma)\geq-2k^{2}+(\beta-4\gamma+2)k+(2\beta\gamma+2\gamma)
.
Therefore, it suffices to prove (*) for
k=k_{0}=\max\{\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil-\gamma,2\}
.
We consider three cases.

•

Case 1:
\beta\leq 4\gamma+5
and
\alpha=\beta
.
In this case
k=k_{0}=2
.
Hence, the right-hand side of (*) is
2\beta\gamma+2\beta-6\gamma-4
. Consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
This case requires
\alpha>\beta
which contradicts the fact that
\alpha=\beta
.

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
This case requires
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8=\beta+6\gamma+8
.
Thus,
\beta<3\gamma+4
.
Therefore, we obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma=2\beta\gamma+\beta-3\gamma\geq 2\beta\gamma+2\beta-6\gamma-4
.

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4=2\beta\gamma+2\beta-6\gamma-4
.

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
This case requires
\alpha+\beta
being odd which contradicts
\alpha=\beta
.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
This case requires
\alpha+\beta
being odd which contradicts
\alpha=\beta
.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
This case requires
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6=\beta+4\gamma+6
which implies
\beta>4\gamma+6
. We arrive at a contradiction with the case assumption
\beta\leq 4\gamma+5
.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
This case requires
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6=\beta+4\gamma+6
which implies
\beta>4\gamma+6
. We arrive at a contradiction with the case assumption
\beta\leq 4\gamma+5
.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
This case requires
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6=\beta+4\gamma+6
which implies
\beta>4\gamma+6
. We arrive at a contradiction with the case assumption
\beta\leq 4\gamma+5
.

•

Case 2:
\beta\leq 4\gamma+5
and
\alpha>\beta
.

In this case
k=k_{0}=2
.
Hence, the right-hand side of (*) is
2\beta\gamma+2\beta-6\gamma-4
.
Now we use the case assumption
\beta\leq 4\gamma+5
and
Lemma

8.23
to obtain
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq(\beta-1)\cdot(2\gamma+1)+2=2\beta\gamma+\beta-2\gamma+1\geq 2\beta\gamma+2\beta-6\gamma-4
.

•

Case 3:
\beta\geq 4\gamma+6
.

In this case
k=k_{0}=\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil-\gamma
.
Let
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
be such that
\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil=\frac{\beta+x}{4}
.
We substitute
k
in the right-hand side of (*):

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}-2(\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil-\gamma)^{2}+(\beta-4\gamma+2)(\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil-\gamma)+(2\beta\gamma+2\gamma)

\displaystyle=-2\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil^{2}+2\gamma^{2}+\beta\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil+\beta\gamma+2\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{4}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=-2(\frac{\beta+x}{4})^{2}+2\gamma^{2}+\beta\cdot\frac{\beta+x}{4}+\beta\gamma+2\frac{\beta+x}{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}.

(**)

We consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
In this case
\beta<\gamma+2
is required which contradicts the case assumption
\beta\geq 4\gamma+6
.

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
. In terms of
\alpha
,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is a linear function with slope
\frac{\beta-1}{2}>0
.
Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is a strictly increasing function in
\alpha
.
In this case
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required, which yields
\alpha\geq 3\beta-6\gamma-7
.
As
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case, we get
\alpha\geq 3\beta-6\gamma-6
.
Furthermore, the case assumption requires
\beta\geq 4\gamma+6
.
Hence,
0<\gamma\leq\frac{\beta-6}{4}
.
Finally,
\frac{\beta^{2}}{4}+\beta+3\geq\frac{9}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}
as
\beta\geq 3
and
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
.
Combining these facts together, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{F}(3\beta-6\gamma-6,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{(3\beta-6\gamma-6)\cdot\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{3\beta-6\gamma-6}{2}-3\gamma

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}+3-\beta\gamma-3\beta

\displaystyle\geq\beta^{2}+3-\beta\cdot\frac{\beta-6}{4}-3\beta

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}-\frac{5\beta}{2}+(\beta+3+\frac{\beta^{2}}{4})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}-\frac{5\beta}{2}+(\frac{9}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8})

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\cdot\frac{\beta-6}{4}+2(\frac{\beta-6}{4})^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8},

thus proving (**).

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
.
In terms of
\alpha
,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is a linear function with slope
\frac{\beta-2}{2}>0
.
Hence,
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is a strictly increasing function in
\alpha
.
In this case
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
is required which yields
\alpha\geq 2\beta-4\gamma-6
.
Furthermore, the case assumption requires
\beta\geq 4\gamma+6
.
Hence,
0<\gamma\leq\frac{\beta-6}{4}
.
Finally, one can verify that
5\geq\frac{x}{2}+\frac{9}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}
for any
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
.
Combining these facts together, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{F}(2\beta-4\gamma-6,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{(2\beta-4\gamma-6)\cdot\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-(2\beta-4\gamma-6)-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}-2\beta-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}-2\beta-2\frac{\beta-6}{4}+2

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}-\frac{5\beta}{2}+5

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}-\frac{5\beta}{2}+(\frac{x}{2}+\frac{9}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8})

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\cdot\frac{\beta-6}{4}+2(\frac{\beta-6}{4})^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8},

thus proving (**).

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
In this case
\beta<2\gamma+3
is required which contradicts the case assumption
\beta\geq 4\gamma+6
.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
The value of
\mathcal{F}
in this case is different from the one in the third case by
-\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+\frac{5}{2}=\frac{\alpha+4\gamma+6-2\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, where the last inequality holds due to the fact that
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
is required in these two cases.
This is the only case assumption that we used to prove the inequality for the third case.
Hence, the same arguments yield (*) but with
-\frac{1}{2}
on the right-hand side.
As the values on both sides of (*) are integers due to
Lemma

8.7
, it follows that (*) holds (since
n\geq m-\frac{1}{2}
for integers
n
and
m
implies
n\geq m
).

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
Here we consider all cases
6
through
8
together.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y
, where

y=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if $\alpha=0\bmod 4$,}\\
\frac{3}{8},&\text{if $\alpha$ is odd,}\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{if $\alpha=2\bmod 4$.}\end{cases}

We claim that
(\alpha-\beta)\gamma+\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}+y\geq 0
holds.
If
\alpha>\beta
, we get
(\alpha-\beta)\gamma+\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}+y\geq 1+\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}\geq 0
, where the last inequality can be checked for all values
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
.
On the other hand, if
\alpha=\beta
, we have
\alpha=\beta\bmod 4
, and thus for each fixed value of
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, we have a fixed value of
y
.
We get that
(\alpha-\beta)\gamma+\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}+y=\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}+y\geq 0
, where the last inequality can be checked for all values
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
and corresponding values of
y
.
Hence, indeed
(\alpha-\beta)\gamma+\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}+y\geq 0
always holds.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y

\displaystyle\geq 2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\frac{\beta}{2}+y

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}+((\alpha-\beta)\gamma+\frac{x^{2}}{8}-\frac{x}{2}+y)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}+\beta\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8},

thus proving (**).

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

Lemma 8.31
.

If
\alpha\geq\beta\geq 3
,
\gamma\geq 1
,
\alpha+\beta
is even, and
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+5
, then
\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

Proof 8.32
.

We consider four cases of the remainder of
\alpha
modulo four.

1.

\alpha=0\bmod 4
.

In this case the right-hand side of the inequality
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+5
is odd, while the left-hand side is even.
Hence, we get
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+4
, and consequently
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+2
.
We thus obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+2)+2}{2}=\frac{\alpha-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

2.

\alpha=1\bmod 4
.

As
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+5
, we get
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+5/2
.
Hence,
\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+5/2)+2}{2}=\frac{\alpha-1-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

3.

\alpha=2\bmod 4
.

As
\alpha
is even in this case, we get that
\beta
is also even because
\alpha+\beta
is even.
Hence,
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma=2\bmod 4
.
Therefore, as we have
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 5
, we obtain
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 2
, and thus
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+1
.
We obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+1)+2}{2}=\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

4.

\alpha=3\bmod 4
.

As
\alpha
is odd in this case, we get that
\beta
is also odd because
\alpha+\beta
is even.
Hence,
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma=3\bmod 4
.
Therefore, as we have
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 5
, we obtain
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 3
, and thus
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+3/2
.
We obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+3/2)+2}{2}=\frac{\alpha+1-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

Lemma 8.33
.

If
\alpha\geq\beta\geq 3
,
\gamma\geq 1
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, then
\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

Proof 8.34
.

We consider four cases of the remainder of
\alpha
modulo four.

1.

\alpha=0\bmod 4
.

As
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, we get
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+3
.
We thus obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+3)+3}{2}=\frac{\alpha-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

2.

\alpha=1\bmod 4
.

As
\alpha
is odd in this case, we get that
\beta
is even because
\alpha+\beta
is odd.
Hence,
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma=3\bmod 4
.
Therefore, as we have
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 6
, we derive
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 3
.
Thus,
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+3/2
.
We obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+3/2)+3}{2}=\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

3.

\alpha=2\bmod 4
.

As
\alpha
is even in this case, we get that
\beta
is odd because
\alpha+\beta
is odd.
Hence,
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma=0\bmod 4
.
Therefore, as we have
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 6
, we derive
2\beta-\alpha-4\gamma\leq 4
, and thus
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+2
.
We obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+2)+3}{2}=\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

4.

\alpha=3\bmod 4
.

In this case the right-hand side of the inequality
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
is odd, while the left-hand side is even.
Hence, we get
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+5
, and consequently
\beta\leq\alpha/2+2\gamma+5/2
.
We thus obtain
\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha/2+2\gamma+5/2)+3}{2}=\frac{\alpha+1-4\gamma}{4}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor
.

Lemma 8.35
.

For
\alpha\geq\beta\geq 3
,
\gamma\geq 1
, and
\ell\geq 2
such that
\ell\geq\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(2\gamma+1+(\beta-2)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell))
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq\gamma+(\alpha-2\ell)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell)+\ell+1
.

Proof 8.36
.

We have
\ell\geq\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(2\gamma+1+(\beta-2)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell))=(\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+2\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1)-(\beta-2)\cdot\ell
.
By rearranging, we obtain
\ell\geq\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+2\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}
.
As
\ell
is an integer,
\ell\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\coloneqq\left\lceil\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+2\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil
follows.

We need to prove

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}\gamma+(\alpha-2\ell)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell)+\ell+1

\displaystyle=-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1).

(*)

The right-hand side of (*) is a quadratic function in
\ell
with a negative coefficient for
\ell^{2}
.
It achieves the maximum value for
\ell=\ell_{\max}\coloneqq\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+1}{4}
.
As
\ell_{\max}
may be fractional, the maximum value over integer values of
\ell
is achieved for the closest integer to
\ell_{\max}
.
That is,
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}\coloneqq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2}{4}\right\rfloor
.
Furthermore, as we have
\ell\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}
and
\ell\geq 2
, the maximum over possible values of
\ell
is achieved for
\ell=\ell_{0}\coloneqq\max\{\ell^{\prime}_{\max},\ell_{\textup{lo}},2\}
.
Hence,
-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)\geq-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)
.
Therefore, it suffices to prove (*) for
\ell=\ell_{0}=\max\{\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2}{4}\right\rfloor,\left\lceil\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+2\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil,2\}
.
We consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
In this case we have

\displaystyle\ell_{\textup{lo}}

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+2\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2+2\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2},

where
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case.
As
\beta<\gamma+2
is required in this case, we get
\beta\leq\gamma+1
.
Hence,
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\gamma+1)+2}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha+2}{4}-\frac{2\gamma+2}{4}\geq\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\geq\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Furthermore,
\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq 2
as
\alpha>\beta
in this case and
\alpha+\beta
is even.
Therefore,
\ell=\ell_{0}=\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}
in this case.
In order to reuse the arguments in later cases, we substitute
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}
in the right-hand side of (*), where
x=2
in this case:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)

\displaystyle=-2(\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2})^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-\frac{1}{2})\beta-\frac{x-1}{2}\alpha-(2x-1)\gamma+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+1).

(**)

This case requires
\beta<\gamma+2
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2+(\beta-\gamma-2)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-\frac{1}{2})\beta-\frac{x-1}{2}\alpha-(2x-1)\gamma+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+1),

thus proving (**).

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\beta-\gamma-2
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{1+(\beta-\gamma-2)}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\beta-\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil
, where
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case. In the conditions for this case we have that either
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
. We consider two cases

(a)

\alpha=\beta
.
We get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\beta-\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\left\lceil\frac{\beta-\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil\leq 1
.
Furthermore, this case requires
3\alpha=3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
Therefore,
\alpha<3\gamma+4
.
Hence,
\alpha\leq 3\gamma+3
.
We obtain
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2}{4}\right\rfloor\leq\left\lfloor\frac{(3\gamma+3)-4\gamma+2}{4}\right\rfloor=\left\lfloor\frac{5-\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor\leq 1
.
Thus,
\ell=\ell_{0}=2
in this case.
From
\alpha\leq 3\gamma+3
we derive
\alpha\leq 4\gamma+5
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma

\displaystyle=2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma

\displaystyle\geq 2\alpha\gamma+\alpha-3\gamma+(\alpha-4\gamma-5)

\displaystyle=-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1),

thus proving (*).

(b)

\alpha>\beta
and
\beta\geq\gamma+2
.

In this case we have
\beta-\gamma-1\geq 1
.
Thus,
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\beta-\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}
.
Note that
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq 2
as
\alpha>\beta
and
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case.

We now prove
\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
If
\alpha\leq 9
, we get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq 2\geq\frac{\alpha-4+2}{4}\geq\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2}{4}\geq\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Otherwise,
\alpha\geq 10
. As
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required in this case, we get
\beta<(\alpha+6\gamma+8)/3
.
Combining these two facts, we obtain

\displaystyle\ell_{\textup{lo}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha-(\alpha+6\gamma+8)/3+2}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2+(\alpha-10)/3}{4}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2}{4}

\displaystyle\geq\ell^{\prime}_{\max}.

Hence, indeed
\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
always holds, and we have
\ell=\ell_{0}=\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}
for
x=2
in this case.
We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-\frac{1}{2})\beta-\frac{x-1}{2}\alpha-(2x-1)\gamma+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+1),

thus proving (**).

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\frac{5}{2}\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-6
.
Thus, we analogously get

\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{1+(\frac{5}{2}\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-6)}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\frac{3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8}{2}+(\beta-\gamma-1)}{\beta-1}\right\rceil\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case, and by the case requirements we have
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8\geq 3\cdot(\gamma+2)
.
We consider two cases.

(a)

\alpha+4\gamma+6=2\beta
.

Hence,
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+6
.
As
\beta
is an integer, we get that
\alpha
is even.
As
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case, we get that
\beta
is also even.
Hence, for the equality
\alpha+4\gamma+6=2\beta
to hold modulo four, we get
\alpha=2\bmod 4
.
Thus, in this case
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}
.
Combining these facts, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle=(\alpha+4\gamma+6)\cdot\gamma+\frac{\alpha\cdot(\alpha/2+2\gamma+3)}{2}-\frac{(\alpha/2+2\gamma+3)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle+3\cdot(\frac{\alpha}{2}+2\gamma+3)-\alpha-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\alpha\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{2\alpha+2}{4}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\alpha\gamma+2\gamma^{2}+\frac{\alpha+4}{4}

\displaystyle=-2(\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4})^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)

\displaystyle=-2\ell^{\prime 2}_{\max}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot\ell^{\prime}_{\max}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)

\displaystyle\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1),

thus proving (*), where the last inequality holds as
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
is the point in which such a quadratic function takes its maximum value over integers.

(b)

\alpha+4\gamma+6\neq 2\beta
.
As
\alpha+4\gamma+6\geq 2\beta
is required in this case, we obtain
\alpha+4\gamma+5\geq 2\beta
.
Thus,
Lemma

8.31
implies
\ell_{0}\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}\geq\ell_{\max}
.
Hence, it is sufficient to prove (*) for
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}
as then
-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)
.
As
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required in this case, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4+(\alpha+6\gamma+8-3\beta)/2

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-\frac{1}{2})\beta-\frac{x-1}{2}\alpha-(2x-1)\gamma+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+1),

thus proving (**) because
x=2
in this case.

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2}
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{1+(\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2})}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha+\beta+1}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is odd in this case.
In this case
\beta<2\gamma+3
is required, thus
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(2\gamma+3)+3}{2}=\frac{2\alpha-4\gamma}{4}\geq\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Furthermore,
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq 2
as
\alpha+\beta
is odd.
Consequently, we get that
\ell=\ell_{0}=\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
in this case.
As
\beta<2\gamma+3
is required in this case,
3\beta<6\gamma+9\leq 6\gamma+7+\alpha
follows.
We thus obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}+(3\beta-6\gamma-7-\alpha)/2

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{5\beta}{2}-\alpha-5\gamma-2

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-\frac{1}{2})\beta-\frac{x-1}{2}\alpha-(2x-1)\gamma+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+1),

thus proving (**) because
x=3
in this case.

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\frac{3}{2}\beta-2\gamma-\frac{7}{2}
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{1+(\frac{3}{2}\beta-2\gamma-\frac{7}{2})}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\beta-2\gamma-2}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha+\beta+1}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is odd in this case, and by the case requirements we have
\beta-2\gamma-2\geq 1
.
Furthermore,
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
due to
Lemma

8.33
.
Finally,
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq 2
as
\alpha+\beta
is odd.
Consequently, we get that
\ell=\ell_{0}=\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
in this case.
As
\alpha\geq\beta
, we derive
\alpha/2+\gamma+1/2\geq\beta/2
.
We thus obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}+(\beta/2-\alpha/2-\gamma-1/2)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{5\beta}{2}-\alpha-5\gamma-2

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-\frac{1}{2})\beta-\frac{x-1}{2}\alpha-(2x-1)\gamma+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+1),

thus proving (**) because
x=3
in this case.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
Here we consider all cases
6
through
8
together.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y
, where

y=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if $\alpha=0\bmod 4$,}\\
\frac{3}{8},&\text{if $\alpha$ is odd,}\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{if $\alpha=2\bmod 4$.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, let
x\in\{-1,0,1,2\}
by such that
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+2}{4}\right\rfloor-\gamma=\frac{\alpha+x}{4}-\gamma
.
We claim that
y+\frac{\alpha}{4}\geq 1+\frac{x}{4}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}
holds.
To prove it, we consider four cases of the remainder of
\alpha
modulo four.

(a)

\alpha=0\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=y=0
and
\alpha\geq 4
. By substitution the inequality holds.

(b)

\alpha=1\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=-1
,
y=3/8
, and
\alpha\geq 3
. By substitution the inequality holds.

(c)

\alpha=2\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=2
,
y=1/2
, and
\alpha\geq 3
. By substitution the inequality holds.

(d)

\alpha=3\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=1
,
y=3/8
, and
\alpha\geq 3
. By substitution the inequality holds.

Thus, indeed we have
y+\frac{\alpha}{4}\geq 1+\frac{x}{4}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y

\displaystyle\geq 2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y+(1+\frac{x}{4}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}-y-\frac{\alpha}{4})

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{4}+(1+\frac{x}{4}-\frac{x^{2}}{8})

\displaystyle=-2(\frac{\alpha+x}{4}-\gamma)^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot(\frac{\alpha+x}{4}-\gamma)+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)

\displaystyle=-2\ell^{\prime 2}_{\max}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot\ell^{\prime}_{\max}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1)

\displaystyle\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+1)\cdot\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+\gamma+1),

thus proving (*), where the last inequality holds as
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
is the point in which such a quadratic function takes its maximum value over integers.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

Lemma 8.37
.

For
\alpha\geq\beta\geq 4
,
\gamma\geq 1
, and
\ell\geq 2
, such that
\ell\geq\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(2\gamma+1+(\beta-4)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell)+2\cdot(\gamma+\ell))
, we have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\geq(\alpha-2\ell+1)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell)+\ell
.

Proof 8.38
.

We have
\ell\geq\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)-(2\gamma+1+(\beta-4)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell)+2\cdot(\gamma+\ell))=(\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+4\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1)-(\beta-2)\cdot\ell
.
By rearranging, we obtain

\ell\geq\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+4\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}
.
As
\ell
is an integer,
\ell\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\coloneqq\left\lceil\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+4\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil
follows.

We need to prove

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}(\alpha-2\ell+1)\cdot(2\gamma+\ell)+\ell

\displaystyle=-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma).

(*)

The right-hand side of (*) is a quadratic function in
\ell
with a negative coefficient for
\ell^{2}
.
It achieves the maximum value for
\ell=\ell_{\max}\coloneqq\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+2}{4}
.
As
\ell_{\max}
may be fractional, the maximum value over integer values of
\ell
is achieved for the closest integer to
\ell_{\max}
.
That is,
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}\coloneqq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+3}{4}\right\rfloor
.
Furthermore, as we have
\ell\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}
and
\ell\geq 2
, the maximum over possible values of
\ell
is achieved for
\ell=\ell_{0}\coloneqq\max\{\ell^{\prime}_{\max},\ell_{\textup{lo}},2\}
.
Hence,
-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)\geq-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)
.
Therefore, it suffices to prove (*) for
\ell=\ell_{0}=\max\{\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha-4\gamma+3}{4}\right\rfloor,\left\lceil\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+4\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil,2\}
.
We consider all the cases from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
.

1.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
\alpha>\beta
, and
\beta<\gamma+2
.
In this case we have

\displaystyle\ell_{\textup{lo}}

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+4\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2+4\gamma-2\beta\gamma-1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\left\lceil\frac{\frac{\beta\cdot(\alpha-\beta)}{2}-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+2\gamma+1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{2\gamma+1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+6}{2},

where
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case, and
\frac{2\gamma+1}{\beta-1}>2
as
\beta\leq\gamma+1
in this case.
Furthermore,
\ell_{0}\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+6}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(\gamma+1)+6}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Hence, it is sufficient to prove (*) for
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+6}{2}
as then
-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)
.
In order to reuse the arguments in later cases, we substitute
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}
in the right-hand side of (*), where
x=6
in this case:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)

\displaystyle=-2(\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2})^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\cdot\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-1)\beta+(1-\frac{x}{2})\alpha+(2-2x)\gamma+(x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}).

(**)

As
\beta<\gamma+2
in this case, we derive
3\alpha/2+8\gamma+14\geq 3\alpha/2+3\cdot(\gamma+2)\geq 3\beta/2+3\beta=9\beta/2
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+2+(9\beta/2-3\alpha/2-8\gamma-14)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+5\beta-2\alpha-10\gamma-12

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-1)\beta+(1-\frac{x}{2})\alpha+(2-2x)\gamma+(x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}),

thus proving (**).

2.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
, and (
\alpha=\beta
or
\beta\geq\gamma+2
).
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\beta-\gamma-2
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{2\gamma+1+(\beta-\gamma-2)}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\beta-1+\gamma}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\gamma}{\beta-1}\right\rceil\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case.
As
3\beta<\alpha+6\gamma+8
is required in this case, we derive
\ell_{0}\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}=\frac{3\alpha-3\beta+12}{6}\geq\frac{3\alpha-(\alpha+6\gamma+8)+12}{6}=\frac{4\alpha+8-12\gamma}{12}\geq\frac{3\alpha+9-12\gamma}{12}=\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Hence, it is sufficient to prove (*) for
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}
where
x=4
in this case, as then
-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)
.
As
\alpha+6\gamma+8>3\beta
is required in this case, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-3\gamma+(3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8)/2

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-1)\beta+(1-\frac{x}{2})\alpha+(2-2x)\gamma+(x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}),

thus proving (**).

3.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4
,
\alpha+\beta
is even,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\frac{5}{2}\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-6
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{2\gamma+1+(\frac{5}{2}\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-6)}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\frac{3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8}{2}+(\beta-1)+\gamma}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+2}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\frac{3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-8}{2}+\gamma}{\beta-1}\right\rceil\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is even in this case, and
3\beta\geq\alpha+6\gamma+8
in this case.

We claim that
\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}=\frac{\alpha+2}{4}+\frac{\alpha+4\gamma+6-2\beta}{4}-\gamma\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3}{4}\right\rfloor-\gamma=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell_{\max}
.
Note that
\alpha+4\gamma+6\geq 2\beta
is required in this case.
If
\alpha+4\gamma+6=2\beta
, then
\alpha
is even, and we have
\frac{\alpha+2}{4}+\frac{\alpha+4\gamma+6-2\beta}{4}-\gamma\geq\frac{\alpha+2}{4}-\gamma\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3}{4}\right\rfloor-\gamma
.
Otherwise,
\alpha+4\gamma+5\geq 2\beta
, and we have
\frac{\alpha+2}{4}+\frac{\alpha+4\gamma+6-2\beta}{4}-\gamma\geq\frac{\alpha+2}{4}+\frac{1}{4}-\gamma\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3}{4}\right\rfloor-\gamma
.
Therefore, indeed
\ell_{0}\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}\geq\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Hence, it is sufficient to prove (*) for
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+4}{2}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}
where
x=4
in this case, as then
-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)
.
We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+3\beta-\alpha-6\gamma-4

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-1)\beta+(1-\frac{x}{2})\alpha+(2-2x)\gamma+(x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}),

thus proving (**).

4.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd, and
\beta<2\gamma+3
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2}
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{2\gamma+1+(\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2})}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{2\gamma+1}{\beta-1}\right\rceil\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is odd in this case.
Furthermore, as
2\gamma+3>\beta
is required in this case, we derive
\ell_{0}\geq\ell_{\textup{lo}}\geq\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\frac{\alpha-(2\gamma+3)+3}{2}=\frac{2\alpha-4\gamma}{4}\geq\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
.
Hence, it is sufficient to prove (*) for
\ell=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+x}{2}
where
x=3
in this case, as then
-2\ell^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)
.

This case requires
2\gamma+3>\beta
to hold. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2\gamma+\frac{3}{2}+(\beta-2\gamma-3)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-1)\beta+(1-\frac{x}{2})\alpha+(2-2x)\gamma+(x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}),

thus proving (**).

5.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}
,
\alpha+\beta
is odd,
2\beta\leq\alpha+4\gamma+6
, and
\beta\geq 2\gamma+3
.
In this case
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
is different from the value in the first case by
\frac{3}{2}\beta-2\gamma-\frac{7}{2}
.
Thus, we analogously get
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\left\lceil\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}+\frac{2\gamma+1+(\frac{3}{2}\beta-2\gamma-\frac{7}{2})}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}+\left\lceil\frac{\beta-2}{\beta-1}\right\rceil=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
, where
\frac{\alpha-\beta+1}{2}
is an integer as
\alpha+\beta
is odd in this case.

Lemma

8.33
implies that
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\ell_{\max}
.
Finally,
\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}\geq 2
as
\alpha+\beta
is odd.
Hence
\ell_{0}=\ell_{\textup{lo}}=\frac{\alpha-\beta+3}{2}
in this case.
We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+2\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}-4\gamma-\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle=2\beta\gamma+\frac{\alpha\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}+(x-1)\beta+(1-\frac{x}{2})\alpha+(2-2x)\gamma+(x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}),

thus proving (**), where
x=3
in this case.

6.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\alpha=0\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
Here we consider all cases
6
through
8
together.
We have
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y
, where

y=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if $\alpha=0\bmod 4$,}\\
\frac{3}{8},&\text{if $\alpha$ is odd,}\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{if $\alpha=2\bmod 4$.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, let
x\in\{0,1,2,3\}
by such that
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3-4\gamma}{4}\right\rfloor=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha+3}{4}\right\rfloor-\gamma=\frac{\alpha+x}{4}-\gamma
.
We claim that
y=\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}
holds.
To prove it, we consider four cases of the remainder of
\alpha
modulo four.

(a)

\alpha=0\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=y=0
. The equality obviously holds.

(b)

\alpha=1\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=3
and
y=3/8
. The equality obviously holds.

(c)

\alpha=2\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=2
and
y=1/2
. The equality obviously holds.

(d)

\alpha=3\bmod 4
.
In this case
x=1
and
y=3/8
. The equality obviously holds.

Thus, indeed we have
y=\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8}
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+y

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+(\frac{x}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{8})

\displaystyle=-2(\frac{\alpha+x}{4}-\gamma)^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\cdot(\frac{\alpha+x}{4}-\gamma)+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)

\displaystyle=-2\ell^{\prime 2}_{\max}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\cdot\ell^{\prime}_{\max}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma)

\displaystyle\geq-2\ell_{0}^{2}+(\alpha-4\gamma+2)\ell_{0}+(2\alpha\gamma+2\gamma),

thus proving (*), where the last inequality holds as
\ell^{\prime}_{\max}
is the point in which such a quadratic function takes its maximum value over integers.

7.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{3}{8}
,
\alpha
is odd, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

8.

\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=2\gamma^{2}+\alpha\gamma+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha=2\bmod 4
, and
2\beta>\alpha+4\gamma+6
.
See case
6
.

Appendix A
Example: Algorithm and Lower Bound for
P(9,2,2)

In this section we show that the pattern graph
P(9,2,2)
has complexity
2-1/8
. This is an example for our algorithms for graphs in
\mathcal{P}
(see
Lemma

2.19
) and for our lower bounds for graphs in
\mathcal{P}
(see
Lemma

2.11
).

As mentioned in the proof overview, algorithms for subgraph finding typically use the high-degree-low-degree idea of Alon, Yuster, and Zwick
[
9
]
(at least in the algorithm theory community). Here we generalize the usual degree splitting to “hyper-degree splitting”, where we have already computed a superset
T
of the projection of all
H
-subgraphs to some vertex parts
V_{1}\times\ldots\times V_{\ell}
and we split the tuples
(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell-1})
into high degree and low degree based on their hyper-degree
\textup{deg}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell-1})=|\{v_{\ell}:(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell})\in T\}|
.
For an example that involves
\ell=3
parts, see Cases 2.1 and 2.2 in the proof of
Lemma

A.1
below.
We remark that for the pattern
P(9,2,2)
the use of hyper-degree splitting seems necessary, since without hyper-degree splitting (i.e., in the classic high-degree-low-degree framework) we were only able to obtain exponent
2-1/9
, while with hyper-degree splitting we obtain the optimal exponent
2-1/8
. We prove the matching conditional lower bound in
Lemma

A.3
below.

Lemma A.1
.

P(9,2,2)
-Encoding can be solved in time
\mathcal{O}(m^{2-\frac{1}{8}})
.

Proof A.2
.

We write
H=P(9,2,2)
.
Consider any
H
-subgraph
\overline{v}
in
G
. We create several partial
H
-encodings of
G
and show that exactly one of them encodes
\overline{v}
.

We split the nodes in
V_{a_{0}},V_{a_{1}},\ldots,V_{a_{9}}
into the ones that have degree less than
m^{\frac{1}{8}}
(low-degree nodes, sets
V_{a_{i}}^{\textup{lo}}
) and the ones that have degree at least
m^{\frac{1}{8}}
(high-degree nodes, sets
V_{a_{i}}^{\textup{hi}}
). For each of the
2^{10}
choices of high or low degrees, we construct several partial
H
-encodings that encode exactly the
H
-subgraphs that satisfy these degree constraints. As
\overline{v}
satisfies exactly one of these
2^{10}
